Care to explain please? Tim I'll tell you. Suppose we have a USDJPY demand zone 30 pips wide. Now, you set limit buy order for every pip in that zone. So, that gives you 30 buy limit orders. Next, you choose a currency that has a high correlation coefficient with the USDJPY - GBPJPY for example. Demand zone for GBPJPY zone is 35 pips wide. You use the same procedure of placing buy limit orders as in the USDJPY. That gives you 35 buy limit orders. Market begins to move, demand level has been hit and penetrated 90% on both pairs. So you end up with 27 buy orders on USDJPY, and 32 orders on GBPJPY. Demand levels holds and the market begins to move in your direction. Finaly, USDJPY is 80 pips up and GBPJPY 120 pips up. And voil�... you made 80x27+120*32= 2.160+3.840 = 5.960 pips in one day. Publicly, you don't talk about the losses in the same manner or about aggressive scaling method so people think you are a superman and they start to buy your "know how" for just $30 like creazy. Toni QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Jiri's NewWay #238 Jiris109 Visitor forexdaist wrote: Jiris109 wrote: starting 4th month, still doing the same over and over again HTF trend + LTF*SBE https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1841-jiri-s-newway?start=225 16/36 7/27/2018 Jiri's NewWay - Page 10 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT HI JIRIS, New here but I followed your post since july and I'm very impressed for your work. I'm still trying to manage S/D stretegy. Your style looks great. Can you de ne what is "SBE", refering to low time frames? Do you mean just picking good demand/supply levels? Which HTF do you recommend to determine the trend if you have 15M as a LTF? Is it 1H ok? Thanks, a good trades! Toni Hello, thank you for a question. SBE is "Snow Ball Effect" In my point of view SBE is technique where you see the HTF zone and you are trading this zone and than all other zones from LTF in HTF trend direction. All positions from LTF are re.entry and you have it all together open and manage them. In the begining I took 18pips on risk 1%. After some time it was 36pips on 1% of account. So this is now my TOP risk level, according this I know for example that my highest lotsize could be 1,0 lot and this is my maximum for any trade. So if the setup looks good, than I trade 1,0lot a if it didn't than I trade it with 0,1 lot and doesn't matter how much pips I give to the SL. So it's not the best way in progress, because if I need SL only 15 pips, that mean I can easily risk 2,0 lot because of the lower pips needed to reach the risk 35pips = 1,0 lot, but I'am not doing that. Well, i don't think it's a mistake of what you did. But you've got to have a plan, of where and when you should exit, or move ur stops. There're 2 types of traders: 1. Let the trade run, with full positions, and don't mind being stopped out and giving the pips back. 2. Take partial profits along the way, and don't mind "missed profits" when final TP is hit. I'm type 2, and it seems like what you're doing now = type 1. There's no right or wrong, just personal preference, and whichever suits the personality of the trader. U know urself better, so it's up to you to choose, which style suits you better. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1841-jiri-s-newway?start=125 24/33 7/27/2018 Jiri's NewWay - Page 6 - RTM Community Best trading to you my twin. hehe. Care to explain please? Tim I'll tell you. Suppose we have a USDJPY demand zone 30 pips wide. Now, you set limit buy order for every pip in that zone. So, that gives you 30 buy limit orders. Next, you choose a currency that has a high correlation coefficient with the USDJPY - GBPJPY for example. Demand zone for GBPJPY zone is 35 pips wide. You use the same procedure of placing buy limit orders as in the USDJPY. That gives you 35 buy limit orders. Market begins to move, demand level has been hit and penetrated 90% on both pairs. So you end up with 27 buy orders on USDJPY, and 32 orders on GBPJPY. Demand levels holds and the market begins to move in your direction. Finaly, USDJPY is 80 pips up and GBPJPY 120 pips up. And voil�... you made 80x27+120*32= 2.160+3.840 = 5.960 pips in one day. Publicly, you don't talk about the losses in the same manner or about aggressive scaling method so people think you are a superman and they start to buy your "know how" for just $30 like creazy. Do you risk 1%, 2%, 5% per trade or is that something that you choose with each setup depending on how good the trade looks? Thanks DT Well this is exactly as you said. In the begining I took 18pips on risk 1%. After some time it was 36pips on 1% of account. So this is now my TOP risk level, according this I know for example that my highest lotsize could be 1,0 lot and this is my maximum for any trade. So if the setup looks good, than I trade 1,0lot a if it didn't than I trade it with 0,1 lot and doesn't matter how much pips I give to the SL. So it's not the best way in progress, because if I need SL only 15 pips, that mean I can easily risk 2,0 lot because of the lower pips needed to reach the risk 35pips = 1,0 lot, but I'am not doing that. Just trade fixed risk and make no calculations during the day This maximum I set every morning, so on monday TOP risk level is 50pips with 1,0 lot and if I make some money, than Thuesday can be 50pips with 1,5lot etc...not weekly or monthly basis, just daily QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1841-jiri-s-newway?start=50 6/30 7/27/2018 Jiri's NewWay - Page 3 - RTM Community Jiri'HsONMeEwWay EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT #55 Jiris109 Visitor Iam on holiday in dog camp, trying made positional trades on my nexus 7. Well, i don't think it's a mistake of what you did. But you've got to have a plan, of where and when you should exit, or move ur stops. There're 2 types of traders: 1. Let the trade run, with full positions, and don't mind being stopped out and giving the pips back. 2. Take partial profits along the way, and don't mind "missed profits" when final TP is hit. I'm type 2, and it seems like what you're doing now = type 1. There's no right or wrong, just personal preference, and whichever suits the personality of the trader. U know urself better, so it's up to you to choose, which style suits you better. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1841-jiri-s-newway?start=125 24/33 7/27/2018 Jiri's NewWay - Page 6 - RTM Community Best trading to you my twin. hehe. Care to explain please? Tim I'll tell you. Suppose we have a USDJPY demand zone 30 pips wide. Now, you set limit buy order for every pip in that zone. So, that gives you 30 buy limit orders. Next, you choose a currency that has a high correlation coefficient with the USDJPY - GBPJPY for example. Demand zone for GBPJPY zone is 35 pips wide. You use the same procedure of placing buy limit orders as in the USDJPY. That gives you 35 buy limit orders. Market begins to move, demand level has been hit and penetrated 90% on both pairs. So you end up with 27 buy orders on USDJPY, and 32 orders on GBPJPY. Demand levels holds and the market begins to move in your direction. Finaly, USDJPY is 80 pips up and GBPJPY 120 pips up. And voil�... you made 80x27+120*32= 2.160+3.840 = 5.960 pips in one day. Publicly, you don't talk about the losses in the same manner or about aggressive scaling method so people think you are a superman and they start to buy your "know how" for just $30 like creazy. SBE is "Snow Ball Effect" In my point of view SBE is technique where you see the HTF zone and you are trading this zone and than all other zones from LTF in HTF trend direction. All positions from LTF are re.entry and you have it all together open and manage them. I remember the excruciating pain of every pip going against me. Entering again, because "it just HAS to retrace from here". Well, i don't think it's a mistake of what you did. But you've got to have a plan, of where and when you should exit, or move ur stops. There're 2 types of traders: 1. Let the trade run, with full positions, and don't mind being stopped out and giving the pips back. 2. Take partial profits along the way, and don't mind "missed profits" when final TP is hit. I'm type 2, and it seems like what you're doing now = type 1. There's no right or wrong, just personal preference, and whichever suits the personality of the trader. I was watching this pair at that time with some S/R lines and 15M supply, where the market was compressing in. What led you to take the first tarde as a BO (it seems to me your first short was a BO trade into compression - correct me pls if not) and let the order left in the market for longer time and not cancel it at the end of CP - point A, which is FTR or at the line below? Step in the market at point X and exit at A was in my mind. X - RT to SR line after rejection from 15M Supply. The second trade you entered in M1 supply, it worked out very well and you aimed lower. From HTF I have no idea, why you entered this trade. For me, the price was coming from bear trap, maybe consuming "your" M1 supply, so another rise may occur, but no HTF help, so no exact direction in the market at that time for me. I would step in at RT (B) after engulfing bear trap aiming to C or the line below - the lowest point of the flag on the left (not on the chart), which was maybe the reason why you exited the second trade there. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1841-jiri-s-newway?start=175 28/49 7/27/2018 Jiri's NewWay - Page 8 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Thank in advance PP15 Attachments: QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Jiri's NewWay #189 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1841-jiri-s-newway?start=175 29/49 7/27/2018 Jiri's NewWay - Page 8 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Jiris109 Visitor PP15 wrote: Hi Jiri, for the first - you have amazing results! :) for the second - I would like to ask you the reasons, why you entered EU short this morning like that.
